\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{1}{\langle\eta\rangle^{\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi_{j}(y)\phi_{j}(y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathbb{E}\big[:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}(y)\!:\,:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}(y^{\prime})\!:\big]

\displaystyle\quad\times e^{-2\pi i\eta\cdot y}e^{-2\pi i\eta^{\prime}\cdot y^{\prime}}dydy^{\prime}e^{2\pi i\eta\cdot x}e^{2\pi i\eta^{\prime}\cdot x}d\eta d\eta^{\prime}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{1}{L^{2m}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{1}{\langle\eta\rangle^{\varepsilon}}\widehat{\phi}_{j}\big(\eta-\tfrac{n}{L}\big)e^{2\pi i\eta\cdot x}d\eta\bigg)\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{1}{\langle\eta^{\prime}\rangle^{\varepsilon}}\widehat{\phi}_{j}\big(\eta^{\prime}+\tfrac{n}{L}\big)e^{2\pi i\eta^{\prime}\cdot x}d\eta^{\prime}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\dots,n_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
n=n_{1}+\cdots+n_{m}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\bigg\{\big(\widehat{\mathcal{S}(t)}\big(\tfrac{n_{j}}{L}\big)\big)^{2}\frac{1-e^{-2\langle\frac{2\pi n_{j}}{L}\rangle^{2}}}{2\langle\frac{2\pi n_{j}}{L}\rangle^{2}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{e^{-mt}}{\langle\frac{x}{2^{j}}\rangle^{M}}\frac{1}{L^{2m}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{1}{\langle\frac{n}{L}\rangle^{2\varepsilon-}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\dots,n_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
n=n_{1}+\cdots+n_{m}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\langle\frac{n_{j}}{L}\rangle^{2}}.

By a Riemann sum approximation, we then obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}&\Big[\big|\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\varepsilon}\big(\phi_{j}:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:\big)(x)\big|^{2}\Big]\\
&\lesssim\frac{e^{-mt}}{\langle\frac{x}{2^{j}}\rangle^{M}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{m}}\frac{1}{\langle y_{1}+\cdots+y_{m}\rangle^{2\varepsilon-}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\langle y_{j}\rangle^{2}}dy_{1}\cdots dy_{m}\\
&\lesssim\frac{e^{-mt}}{\langle\frac{x}{2^{j}}\rangle^{M}}\end{aligned}

(4.55)

for any
M\gg 1
, uniformly in
L\geq 1
.

We now proceed as in (
4.51
).
Namely,
given any finite
q\geq p\geq 2
,
it follows from
(
2.22
), Minkowski’s integral inequality,
the Wiener chaos estimate (Lemma
2.12
),
and (
4.55
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\|:\!(S(t)&Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p}_{\mu}}\big\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim q^{\frac{m}{2}}\Big\|w_{\frac{\mu}{p}}(2^{j})\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\varepsilon}\big(\phi_{j}:\!(S(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:\big)(x)\big\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\Big\|_{\ell^{p}_{j}L^{p}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim q^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{-\frac{m}{2}t}\bigg\|w_{\frac{\mu}{p}}(2^{j})\frac{1}{\langle\frac{x}{2^{j}}\rangle^{\frac{M}{2}}}\bigg\|_{\ell^{p}_{j}L^{p}_{x}}\lesssim q^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{-\frac{m}{2}t}\|w_{\frac{\mu}{p}}(2^{j})2^{\frac{2}{p}j}\|_{\ell^{p}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim q^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{-\frac{m}{2}t},\end{split}

(4.56)

uniformly in
L\geq 1
.

We briefly discuss how to handle the time increment

:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:-:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:\,
.
The main idea is to proceed as in the second half of
the proof of Proposition 2.1 in
[
38
]
.
Given
h\in\mathbb{R}
, define the difference operator

\delta_{h}
by setting
29
29

29

We assume that
t+h\geq 0
.

\delta_{h}F(t)=F(t+h)-F(t).

Then, by Lemma
2.13
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m!}\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\big[\delta_{h}:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}(y)\!:\,\delta_{h}:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}(y^{\prime})\!:\big]

\displaystyle=\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))(y)\cdot(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))(y^{\prime})\big]\Big\}^{m}

\displaystyle\quad-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y)\cdot(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))(y^{\prime})\big]\Big\}^{m}

\displaystyle\quad-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))(y)\cdot(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y^{\prime})\big]\Big\}^{m}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y)\cdot(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y^{\prime})\big]\Big\}^{m}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\delta_{h}(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y)\cdot(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))(y^{\prime})\big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))(y)\cdot(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))(y^{\prime})\big]\Big\}^{m-j-1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y)\cdot(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))(y^{\prime})\big]\Big\}^{j}

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\big[\delta_{h}(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y)\cdot(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y^{\prime})\big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))(y)\cdot(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y^{\prime})\big]\Big\}^{m-j-1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y)\cdot(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))(y^{\prime})\big]\Big\}^{j}

Note that in each term on the right-hand side above,
the difference operator
\delta_{h}
appears exactly once.
Then, with (
4.52
), we can repeat the computation above
for
:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:\,

and obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\|\delta_{h}:\!(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p}_{\mu}}\big\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}&\lesssim q^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{-\frac{m}{2}t}|h|^{\kappa}\end{split}

(4.57)

for any
q\geq 1

and
0<\kappa<\varepsilon
,
uniformly in
L\geq 1
.

Therefore,
applying
Kolmogorov’s continuity criterion
with (
4.56
) and (
4.57
)
and arguing as in the
m=1
case
with Lemma
2.2

and the Arzelà-Ascoli theorem,
we conclude that
the family
\{:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:\}_{L\geq 1}

in
C(\mathbb{R}_{+};W^{-\varepsilon,p}_{\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))

is tight.

\bullet

Part 2:

Next, we consider

(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1))^{m}
,
m=1,\dots,k
.
By the fractional Leibniz rule (Lemma
2.11
(i)),
(
2.24
), and Lemma

, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1))^{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}W^{\varepsilon,p}_{\frac{\mu}{2^{\delta}}}}&\lesssim\|\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1)\|^{m}_{L^{\infty}_{t}W^{\varepsilon,mp}_{\mu_{1}}}\lesssim\|\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1)\|^{m}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1-\frac{2}{mp}+\varepsilon}_{\mu_{2}}}\\
&\lesssim\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}e^{-\frac{m}{4}t}\|Y_{L}(1)\|^{m}_{H^{1-\frac{2}{mp}+\varepsilon}_{\mu_{3}}}\leq\|Y_{L}(1)\|^{m}_{H^{1-\frac{2}{mp}+\varepsilon}_{\mu_{3}}}\end{split}

(4.58)

for some
0<\mu_{3}<\mu_{2}<\mu_{1}<\mu
.
Here, we take
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small such that
1-\frac{2}{mp}+\varepsilon<1
.
It follows from
Proposition
3.3

with (
3.7
) and (
3.29
), Lemma
2.12
,
and
[
55
, Theorem 5.1]
30
30

30

Theorem 5.4 in the arXiv version.

that, given any finite
q\geq 1
,
the
q
th moment of the right-hand side of (
4.58
)
is bounded, uniformly in
L\geq 1
.

A similar computation with (
4.52
) (but with
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
)
yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\delta_{h}(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1))^{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}W^{\varepsilon,p}_{\frac{\mu}{2^{\delta}}}}\lesssim|h|^{\kappa}\|Y_{L}(1)\|^{m}_{H^{1-\frac{2}{mp}+\varepsilon+\kappa}_{\mu_{3}}}.\end{split}

(4.59)

Then,
as long as
\varepsilon,\kappa>0
are sufficiently small such that
1-\frac{2}{mp}+\varepsilon+\kappa<1
,
Proposition
3.3

with (
3.7
) and (
3.29
), Lemma
2.12
,
and
[
55
, Theorem 5.1]
31
31

31

Theorem 5.4 in the arXiv version.

that, given any finite
q\geq 1
,
the
q
th moment of the right-hand side of (
4.59
)
is bounded, uniformly in
L\geq 1
.
Therefore,
applying
Kolmogorov’s continuity criterion
with (

) and (
4.59
)
and arguing as in the

case
with Lemma
2.2

and the Arzelà-Ascoli theorem,
we conclude that
the family

\{(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1))^{m}\}_{L\geq 1}

in
C(\mathbb{R}_{+};W^{\varepsilon,p}_{\frac{\mu}{2^{\delta}}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))

is tight.

\bullet

Part 3:

Finally, from (
4.39
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta_{h}:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)X_{L}(1))^{\ell}\!:\,&=\sum_{m=0}^{\ell}\binom{\ell}{m}\delta_{h}(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1))^{\ell-m}\cdot:\!(\mathcal{S}(t+h)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:\\
&\quad+\sum_{m=0}^{\ell}\binom{\ell}{m}(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1))^{\ell-m}\cdot\delta_{h}:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:.\end{split}

(4.60)

By applying Lemma
2.11
(ii) as in (
4.40
),
we conclude from (
4.51
), (
4.53
), (
4.56
), (
4.57
).
(
4.58
), and (
4.59
)
with
Kolmogorov’s continuity criterion
(
[
5
, Theorem 8.2]
),
Lemma
2.2
,
and the Arzelà-Ascoli theorem
that
the family
\{:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)X_{L}(1))^{m}\!:\}_{L\geq 1}

in
C(\mathbb{R}_{+};W^{-\varepsilon,p}_{\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))

is tight.
Namely,
as probability measures on
\mathbb{W}=C(\mathbb{R}_{+};W^{-\varepsilon,p}_{\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))^{\otimes k}
,
the family
\{\nu_{L}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight.
∎

Let

\mathcal{A}
be the index set
such that Theorem
1.2
(i) holds.
Namely,
that
the
L
-periodic
\Phi^{k+1}_{2}
-measures
\rho_{L}
,
L\in\mathcal{A}
converges weakly to a
limiting
\Phi^{k+1}_{2}
-measure
\rho_{\infty}
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Note that Proposition
4.4

also applies to the family
\{\nu_{L}\}_{L\in\mathcal{A}}
,
showing that there exists a subsequence
which converges, weakly and also in the Wasserstein-1 metric, to
some
limit
\nu_{\infty}
.
The next proposition identifies
this limiting probability measure
\nu_{\infty}
.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let the index set
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathbb{N}
be as in Theorem
1.2
(i).
Then, as probability measures on

\mathbb{W}=\big(C(\mathbb{R}_{+};W^{-\varepsilon,p}_{\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))\big)^{\otimes k}
,
the entire sequence
\{\nu_{L}\}_{L\in\mathcal{A}}

of the
L
-periodic enhanced Gibbs measures
converges, weakly and also in the Wasserstein-1 metric, to
the unique limit

\displaystyle\nu_{\infty}=(\Xi_{0})_{\#}\rho_{\infty}.

(4.61)

Proof.

In the following, we only consider the values of
L
belonging to
\mathcal{A}=\{L_{j}:j\in\mathbb{N}\}\subset\mathbb{N}
.
For simplicity of notation,
we drop the subscript
and simply write
L\to\infty
with the understanding that each
L
belongs to
\mathcal{A}
.

In view of the tightness of
\{\nu_{L}\}_{L\in\mathcal{A}}
(Proposition
4.4
)
and Lemma
2.15
,
we only need to show that the limit in law
of
\{\nu_{L}\}_{L\in\mathcal{A}}

is unique.

Let
X_{L}=Y_{L}+Z_{L}
be the solution to
the
{L}
-periodic SNLH (
3.42
),
where
Y_{L}
and
Z_{L}
satisfy (
3.45
) and (
3.44
)
with the Gibbsian initial data
X_{0,L}
with

\Law(X_{0,L})=\rho_{L}
.
Here, we assume that
X_{0,L}
is independent of the noise
\xi_{L}
.
In view of the weak convergence of
\rho_{L}

to
\rho_{\infty}
as
L\to\infty

(with
L
’s belonging to
\mathcal{A}
)
established
in Subsection
3.3
,
it follows from
the Skorokhod representation theorem (Lemma
2.19
)
that
there exist a probability measure
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
(on a new probability space) and random variables

\widetilde{X}_{0,L}
with
\Law(\widetilde{X}_{0,L})=\rho_{L}
,
L\in\mathcal{A}
,
such that
\widetilde{X}_{0,L}
converges
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely to some
\widetilde{X}_{0,\infty}
, with

\Law(\widetilde{X}_{0,\infty})=\rho_{\infty}
,
in
B^{-\varepsilon,\mu}_{p_{0},p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for any
\varepsilon>0
, finite
p_{0}\geq 1
, and
\mu>0
;
see also Remark
3.6
.
Note that by the independence of
X_{0,L}
and
\xi_{L}
,
the change of the probability spaces due to
the use of the Skorokhod representation theorem (Lemma
2.19
)
does not affect the noise
\xi_{L}
(and hence
Z_{L}
).

Let
\widetilde{X}_{L}=\widetilde{Y}_{L}+Z_{L}
be the solution to (
3.42
)
with the new initial data
\widetilde{X}_{0,L}
,
where
\widetilde{Y}_{L}
satisfies (
3.45
) with the initial data
X_{0,L}
replaced by
\widetilde{X}_{0,L}
.
By the invariance of
\rho_{L}
under (
3.42
), we have

\Law(\widetilde{X}_{L}(1))=\rho_{L}
.
Thus, it suffices to
show that

(i)

\Xi_{0}(\widetilde{X}_{L}(1))

converges in law to

\Xi_{0}(\widetilde{X}_{\infty}(1))

as
L\to\infty
(with
L\in\mathcal{A}
),
where
\widetilde{X}_{\infty}
is the solution to (
3.1
)
with the initial data
\widetilde{X}_{0,\infty}
satisfying
\Law(\widetilde{X}_{0,\infty})=\rho_{\infty}
,
and
\Xi_{0}(\phi)
is the enhanced data set defined in (
4.12
),
and

(ii)

\Law(\widetilde{X}_{\infty}(1))=\rho_{\infty}
.
See Remark
4.7
below.

Before proceeding further, we recall the decomposition
\widetilde{X}_{\infty}=\widetilde{Y}_{\infty}+Z_{\infty}
,
where
\widetilde{Y}_{\infty}
is the solution to (
3.4
)
with the initial data
\widetilde{X}_{0,\infty}

and
Z_{\infty}
is the solution to (
3.2
).
For simplicity of notation, we denote
\widetilde{X}_{L}
,
\widetilde{Y}_{L}
, etc. by
X_{L}
,
Y_{L}
, etc. in the remaining part of the proof.

Given
R>0
,
let
B_{R}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
denotes the ball of radius
R
centered at the origin.
In view of the tightness of
\{\nu_{L}\}_{L\in\mathcal{A}}
(Proposition
4.4
),
it suffices to show that, given any
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
R>0
,

:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)X_{L}(1))^{\ell}\!:
converges in probability to

:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)X_{\infty}(1))^{\ell}\!:

in
W^{-\varepsilon,p}(B_{R})

as
L\to\infty
(with
L\in\mathcal{A}
).
Then, for each fixed
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
this allows us to identify
:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)X_{\infty}(1))^{\ell}\!:
with the limit in law of

:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)X_{L}(1))^{\ell}\!:
as a
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
-valued random variable
by testing against
a test function
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{2})=C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

with
\supp\varphi\subset B_{R}
.
From this observation and Proposition
4.4

with the uniqueness of a limit,
we then conclude
that
the entire sequence

\{\Xi_{0}(X_{L}(1))\}_{L\in\mathcal{A}}
converges in law to

\Xi_{0}(X_{\infty}(1))

as
L\to\infty
(with
L\in\mathcal{A}
).

Remark 4.7
.

Once we prove (i) in the sense explained above,
the claim (ii) above follows immediately.
On the one hand,
as probability measures on
B^{-\varepsilon,\mu}_{p_{0},p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,

\rho_{L}=\Law(X_{L}(1))

converges weakly to
\rho_{\infty}
as
L\to\infty
(with
L\in\mathcal{A})
.
On the other hand, we will show that, given
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\mathcal{S}(t)X_{L}(1)
converges in law to

\mathcal{S}(t)X_{\infty}(1)
as
L\to\infty
(with
L\in\mathcal{A})
.
With
t=0
,
this implies that,
as probability measures on

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,

\rho_{L}=\Law(X_{L}(1))
converges weakly to

\Law(X_{\infty}(1))
.
Therefore, from the uniqueness of a limit, we conclude that

\Law(X_{\infty}(1))=\rho_{\infty}
.

Note that a similar argument shows that

\Law(X_{\infty}(t))=\rho_{\infty}
for any
t>0
,
thus yielding invariance of the limiting
\Phi^{k+1}_{2}
-measure
\rho_{\infty}
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}

under the dynamics of the parabolic
\Phi^{k+1}_{2}
-model (
3.1
)
claimed in Remark
1.5
.

Fix
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
R>0
in the remaining part of the proof.
From Lemma
2.8
(ii) (see also Remark
2.9
) with (
4.38
) and (
4.39
),
we have

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)X_{L}(1))^{\ell}\!:\,-:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)X_{L^{\prime}}(1))^{\ell}\!:\,\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p}(B_{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m=0}^{\ell}\binom{\ell}{m}\Big\{\|:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:-:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L^{\prime}}(1))^{m}\!:\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p_{1}}(B_{R})}

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\|(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1))^{\ell-m}\|_{W^{\varepsilon,p_{2}}(B_{R})}

(4.62)

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXXX}+\|:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L^{\prime}}(1))^{m}\!:\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p_{1}}(B_{R})}

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\|(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1))^{\ell-m}-(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L^{\prime}}(1))^{\ell-m}\|_{W^{\varepsilon,p_{2}}(B_{R})}\Big\}

for any
L,L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
,
where
1<p_{1},p_{2}<\infty

with
p_{1}\gg 1
,
satisfying

In the following, we separately study

\bullet

Part 1:

Let us first study the terms involving

Proceeding as in (

) with

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1))^{\ell-m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}W_{x}^{\varepsilon,p_{2}}(B_{R})}&\lesssim\|\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1)\|^{\ell-m}_{L^{\infty}_{t}W_{x}^{\varepsilon,(\ell-m)p_{2}}(B_{R})}\\

&\lesssim\|Y_{L}(1)\|^{\ell-m}_{H^{1-\frac{2}{(\ell-m)p_{2}}+\varepsilon}(B_{R})}.\end{split}

(4.63)

Then, by Hölder’s inequality and (

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|(\mathcal{S}(t)Y_{L}(1))^{\ell-m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}W_{x}^{\varepsilon,p_{2}}(B_{R})}&\leq C(R)\|Y_{L}(1)\|^{\ell-m}_{B^{1-\frac{2}{(\ell-m)p_{2}}+2\varepsilon}_{p_{3},\infty}(B_{R})}\end{split}

A slight modification of (

&\leq C(R)\Big(\|Y_{L}(1)\|^{\ell-m-1}_{B^{1-\frac{2}{(\ell-m)p_{2}}+2\varepsilon}_{p_{3},\infty}(B_{R})}+\|Y_{L^{\prime}}(1)\|^{\ell-m-1}_{B^{1-\frac{2}{(\ell-m)p_{2}}+2\varepsilon}_{p_{3},\infty}(B_{R})}\Big)\\
&\quad\times\|Y_{L}(1)-Y_{L^{\prime}}(1)\|_{B^{1-\frac{2}{(\ell-m)p_{2}}+2\varepsilon}_{p_{3},\infty}(B_{R})}\end{split}

for any

Given

,

Part 2:

In view of (

) and the discussion above,

We point out that in showing (

:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)X_{L}(1))^{\ell}\!:

in

{W^{-\varepsilon,p}(B_{R})}

(with

)

,

, and each

Hence, it remains to prove (

Fix

and

.
Given

,

,

by

\displaystyle\begin{split}K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)&=\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1)\big)(x)\big(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L^{\prime}}(1)\big)(y)\big]\\
&=K_{L,L^{\prime}}(y,x;t).\end{split}

(4.70)

Then, from Lemma
2.13
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}(x)\!:\,:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L^{\prime}}(1))^{m}(y)\!:\big]=m!\big\{K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}.

(4.71)

Given
a test function
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

supported on a ball
B
of radius 1 with
B\subset B_{2R}
, we set

\displaystyle K^{m,\varphi}_{L,L^{\prime}}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[|\langle:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:-:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L^{\prime}}(1))^{m}\!:\,,\varphi\rangle|^{2}\Big],

(4.72)

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
-
\mathcal{D}
duality pairing.
Then, from (
4.71
) and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K^{m,\varphi}_{L,L^{\prime}}(t)&=m!\iint_{(B_{2R})^{2}}\Big(\big\{K_{L,L}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}-2\big\{K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXX}+\big\{K_{L^{\prime},L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}\Big)\varphi(x)\varphi(y)dxdy\\
&\lesssim\big\|\big\{K_{L,L}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}-2\big\{K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXX}+\big\{K_{L^{\prime},L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}\big\|_{L^{q}_{x,y}(B_{2R})}\|\varphi\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}}^{2}\end{aligned}

(4.73)

for any
1\leq q\leq\infty
with
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=1
.

Now, set

K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)=\lim_{L,L^{\prime}\to\infty}K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)
; see (
4.95
) below for the precise definition.
We then claim that there exists
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)|+|K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)|&\lesssim 1+(-\log|x-y|)_{+},\\
|K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)-K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)|&\lesssim e^{-cL}+e^{-cL^{\prime}}\end{aligned}

(4.74)

for any
L,L^{\prime}\gg R+t
and
x,y\in B_{2R}
,
where

a_{+}=\max(a,0)
.

For now, we assume (
4.74
)
and prove (
4.69
).
By the triangle inequality and (
4.74
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|&\big\{K_{L,L}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}-2\big\{K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}+\big\{K_{L^{\prime},L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}\big|\\
&\leq\big|\big\{K_{L,L}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}-\big\{K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}\big|\\
&\quad+2\big|\big\{K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}-\big\{K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}\big|\\
&\quad+\big|\big\{K_{L^{\prime},L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}-\big\{K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)\big\}^{m}\big|\\
&\lesssim\big(1+(-\log|x-y|)_{+}\big)^{m-1}\big(e^{-cL}+e^{-cL^{\prime}}\big).\end{aligned}

(4.75)

Hence,
by choosing
q=q(\varepsilon)\gg 1

and applying Lemma
2.14

with
(
4.72
), (
4.73
), and (
4.75
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}&\Big[\|:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:-:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L^{\prime}}(1))^{m}\!:\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p_{1}}(B_{R})}^{r}\Big]\\
&\leq C(R,r)\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|\varphi\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}}\leq 1\\
\supp(\varphi)\subset B\subset B_{2R}\end{subarray}}\big(K^{m,\varphi}_{L,L^{\prime}}(t)\big)^{\frac{r}{2}}\\
&\lesssim C(R,r)\big(e^{-cL}+e^{-cL^{\prime}}\big),\end{split}

(4.76)

which tends to zero as
L,L^{\prime}\to\infty
.
This shows that

:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:

converges to
some limit
in
L^{r}(\Omega;W^{-\varepsilon,p_{1}}(B_{R}))

as
L\to\infty
.
By repeating an analogous argument with
L^{\prime}=\infty
,
we obtain (
4.69
).
See Remark
4.8
below.

The remaining part of the proof is devoted to proving the claim (
4.74
);
see Remark
4.8
below
for the case
L^{\prime}=\infty
.
Let
\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
Then, from (
4.43
) with (
4.4
) and (
4.5
), we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1),\varphi\rangle=\sqrt{2}\xi\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(t^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}P(1-t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)\varphi^{\text{\rm per}}_{L}\Big),

where

\varphi^{\text{\rm per}}_{L}
is the
L
-periodized version of
\varphi
defined by

\displaystyle\varphi^{\text{\rm per}}_{L}(x)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi(x+nL).

(4.77)

Given
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we then have

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1)\big)(x)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1),\delta_{x}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\xi\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(t^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}P(1-t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L}\Big),

where
\delta_{x}
denotes the Dirac delta function at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
and hence we can rewrite (
4.70
) as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)&=2\mathbb{E}\bigg[\xi\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(t^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}P(1-t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L}\Big)\\
&\hphantom{XXX}\times\xi\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(t^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}P(1-t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{y})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L^{\prime}}\Big)\bigg]\\
&=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}P(t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L}\Big)(z)\\
&\hphantom{XXX}\times\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}P(t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{y})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L^{\prime}}\Big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz.\end{aligned}

(4.78)

In the following,
we assume
x,y\in B_{2R}
.
By the finite speed of propagation, we have

\displaystyle\supp\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x+nL}\subset x+nL+B_{t}.

(4.79)

Let
\{\psi_{j}^{L}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
be
a smooth partition of unity on
\mathbb{R}^{2}

such that

\psi_{0}^{L}=1
on
B_{\frac{L}{8}}
,

\supp\psi_{0}^{L}\subset B_{\frac{L}{4}}
,
and

\displaystyle\supp\psi_{j}^{L}\subset\big\{\max\big((j-1),\tfrac{1}{8}\big)L\leq|z|\leq(j+1)L\big\}.

(4.80)

In particular, for
j=0
,
we set

\psi_{0}^{L}(z)=\psi_{0}\big(\frac{z}{L}\big)
for some
\psi_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
Here, we choose

\{\psi_{j}^{L}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
such that their first and second derivatives are
uniformly bounded.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}P(t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L}=e^{-t^{\prime}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}\big\{(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L})\big\},

where
p_{t^{\prime}}
is as in (
2.6
).
Under the assumption
L\gg R+t
and
x\in B_{2R}
,
it follows from (
4.77
) and (
4.79
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}(z)\cdot\big\{(\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L})\big\}(z)&=e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x})(z)\\
&=e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathcal{S}(t)(\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})(z-x).\end{split}

(4.81)

By the boundedness of
\mathcal{S}(t)
on
H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and

\psi_{0}^{L}(z)=\psi_{0}\big(\frac{z}{L}\big)
for some
\psi_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\eqref{KK5}\|_{H^{-1-\varepsilon}_{z}}&\lesssim e^{-t^{\prime}}\|\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}}\|_{H^{-1-\varepsilon}}\\
&\lesssim e^{-t^{\prime}}\bigg\|\int_{\eta=\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}}L^{2}\widehat{\psi}_{0}(L\eta_{1})e^{-4\pi^{2}t^{\prime}|\eta_{2}|^{2}}d\eta_{1}\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta}}\\
&\lesssim e^{-t^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{L^{2}}{\langle L\eta_{1}\rangle^{100}}d\eta_{1}\lesssim e^{-t^{\prime}},\end{split}

(4.82)

uniformly in
x\in B_{2R}
,
t\geq 0
, and
t^{\prime}\in[0,1]

with
R+t\ll L
.

For
j\in\mathbb{N}
, it follows from (
4.77
) and (
4.80
)
with
L\gg R+t
and
x\in B_{2R}
that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}(z)\cdot\big\{(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L})\big\}(z)\\
&\quad=e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}(z)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
|n|=j+O(1)\end{subarray}}\bigg\{(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*\Big(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x+nL}\Big)\bigg\}(z)\\
&\quad=e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}(z)\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\\
|n|=j+O(1)\end{subarray}}\mathcal{S}(t)(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})(z-x-nL)\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXXXXXX}+\mathbf{1}_{\{|z|\geq\frac{L}{16}\}}\mathcal{S}(t)(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})(z-x)\bigg\},\end{aligned}

(4.83)

where the second term of the last factor is relevant only for
j=1
.
Note that, for

|z|\geq\frac{L}{16}
and

x\in B_{2R}
with
L\gg R+t
,
we have
|z-x|\geq\frac{L}{32}
.
From the definition of
\psi_{j}^{L}
and (
2.6
),
we then have

\displaystyle\|\eqref{KK6}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim je^{-t^{\prime}}\|\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}_{z}(|z|\geq\frac{L}{32})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}\exp\big(-c\frac{j^{2}L^{2}}{t^{\prime}}-t^{\prime}\big),&\text{for $0<t^{\prime}\ll jL$},\\
\exp(-cjL),&\text{for $t^{\prime}\sim jL$},\\
\exp(-ct^{\prime}),&\text{for $t^{\prime}\gg jL$}.\end{cases}

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\eqref{KK6}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\lesssim e^{-cjL},

(4.84)

uniformly in
x\in B_{2R}
,
t\geq 0
, and
t^{\prime}\in[0,1]

with
R+t\ll L
.

Let
\chi_{L}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2};[0,1])

be a smooth cutoff function
such that
\chi_{L}\equiv 1
on
\big[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2}\big)^{2}

and
\supp\chi_{L}\subset\big[-\frac{(1+\gamma)L}{2},\frac{(1+\gamma)L}{2}\big)^{2}

for some small
\gamma>0
.
We assume that
the first and second derivatives of
\chi_{L}
are
bounded uniformly in
L\gg 1
.
Define
F(z)=F_{j,L,x,t,t^{\prime}}(z)
by

\displaystyle F(z)=F_{j,L,x,t,t^{\prime}}(z)=e^{-t^{\prime}}\chi(z)\cdot\big\{(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L})\big\}(z).

Then, a similar computation
with the uniform boundedness of the first and second derivatives of
\psi_{j}^{L}

shows that

\displaystyle\|F_{j,L,x,t,t^{\prime}}\|_{H^{2}_{z}}\lesssim\|F_{j,L,x,t,t^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}+\|\Delta_{z}F_{j,L,x,t,t^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\lesssim e^{-cjL},

(4.85)

uniformly in
x\in B_{2R}
,
t\geq 0
, and
t^{\prime}\in[0,1]

with
R+t\ll L
.

In view of the discussion above
(in particular, see (
4.81
)),
we write

K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)
in (
4.78
)
as

\displaystyle K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)=K_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(0)}(x,y;t)+K_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(1)}(x,y;t),

(4.86)

where

K_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(0)}(x,y;t)
is defined by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(0)}(x,y;t)&=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x})\Big)(z)\\
&\hphantom{XXX}\times\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{0}^{L^{\prime}}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y})\Big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz\end{aligned}

(4.87)

and

K_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(1)}(x,y;t)=K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)-K_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(0)}(x,y;t)
.
Then, from (
4.78
) and (
4.87
), we have

\displaystyle K_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(1)}(x,y;t)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}\,e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L})\Big)(z)

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}\,e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{j^{\prime}}^{L^{\prime}}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{y})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L^{\prime}})\Big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz

\displaystyle\quad+2\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L})\Big)(z)

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{0}^{L^{\prime}}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y})\Big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz

\displaystyle\quad+2\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x})\Big)(z)

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{j^{\prime}}^{L^{\prime}}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{y})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L^{\prime}})\Big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz

\displaystyle=:\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt(x,y;t)+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(x,y;t)+\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}(x,y;t).

(4.88)

Note that, in view of (
4.81
),
we dropped the cutoff function
\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}

(and
\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}
)
in
I I
(and in
I I I
, respectively).
By Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality with (
4.83
) and (
4.84
), we have

\displaystyle|\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt(x,y;t)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}e^{-cjL}e^{-cj^{\prime}L^{\prime}}\lesssim e^{-c(L+L^{\prime})}.

(4.89)

Next, we estimate
I I
and
I I I
in (
4.88
).
Without loss of generality, assume
L\geq L^{\prime}
.
As for
I I
,
we first insert
\chi_{L}

and then
drop the cutoff function
\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}

in view of
(
4.81
).
Thus, from (
4.82
) and (
4.85
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(x,y;t)|&=2\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}\chi_{L}(z)(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L})\Big)(z)\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{0}^{L^{\prime}}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y})\Big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{1}\big\|e^{-t^{\prime}}\chi_{L}(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L})\big\|_{H^{2}_{z}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\|e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{0}^{L^{\prime}}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y})\|_{H^{-1-\varepsilon}_{z}}dt^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim e^{-cL}.\end{split}

(4.90)

As for the term
I I I
,
we can not drop the cutoff function

\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}

under the current assumption
L\geq L^{\prime}
,
and thus we need to proceed with more care.
We first note that by the mean value theorem, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}\big)(\eta)|\lesssim\frac{(L^{\prime})^{2}}{\langle\eta^{1}\rangle\langle\eta^{2}\rangle},

(4.91)

where
\eta=(\eta^{1},\eta^{2})
.
Then, proceeding as in (
4.82
)
with the triangle inequality
\langle\eta_{1}\rangle^{\varepsilon}\lesssim\langle\eta\rangle^{\varepsilon}\langle\eta_{2}\rangle^{\varepsilon}\langle\eta_{3}\rangle^{\varepsilon}
,
Young’s inequality, and
(
4.91
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|&\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}(z)\mathcal{S}(t)(\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})(z-x)\big\|_{H^{-1-2\varepsilon}_{z}}\\
&\lesssim\bigg\|\frac{1}{\langle\eta\rangle^{1+2\varepsilon}}\int_{\eta=\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}+\eta_{3}}|\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}\big)(\eta_{1})|L^{2}|\widehat{\psi}_{0}(L\eta_{2})|e^{-4\pi^{2}t^{\prime}|\eta_{3}|^{2}}d\eta_{1}d\eta_{2}\bigg\|_{L^{2}_{\eta}}\\
&\lesssim\bigg\|\frac{1}{\langle\eta\rangle^{1+\varepsilon}}\int_{\eta=\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}+\eta_{3}}\langle\eta_{1}\rangle^{-\varepsilon}|\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}\big)(\eta_{1})|\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXXXXXX}\times\langle\eta_{2}\rangle^{\varepsilon}L^{2}|\widehat{\psi}_{0}(L\eta_{2})|\langle\eta_{3}\rangle^{\varepsilon}e^{-4\pi^{2}t^{\prime}|\eta_{3}|^{2}}d\eta_{1}d\eta_{2}\bigg\|_{L^{2}_{\eta}}\\
&\lesssim(L^{\prime})^{2}(t^{\prime})^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}.\end{split}

(4.92)

Hence, from (
4.85
) and (
4.92
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}(x,y;t)|&=2\bigg|\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L^{\prime}}{2},\frac{L^{\prime}}{2})^{2}}(z)\mathcal{S}(t)(\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})(z-x)\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}\chi_{L^{\prime}}(z)(\psi_{j^{\prime}}^{L^{\prime}}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{y})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L^{\prime}})\Big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz\bigg|\\
&\lesssim(L^{\prime})^{2}e^{-cL^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{1}(t^{\prime})^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}dt^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim e^{-c^{\prime}L^{\prime}}.\end{split}

(4.93)

Therefore, from (
4.88
), (
4.89
), (
4.90
), and (
4.93
),
we obtain

\displaystyle|K_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(1)}(x,y;t)|\lesssim e^{-cL}+e^{-cL^{\prime}}.

(4.94)

Next, we study
K^{(0)}_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)

for
L,L^{\prime}\gg 1
.
Define
K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)
by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)&=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\big(P(t^{\prime})(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x}\big)(z)\big(P(t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y}\big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz\\
&=\big\langle(1-\Delta)^{-1}(1-e^{-2(1-\Delta)})\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x},\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{z}}.\end{aligned}

(4.95)

From (
4.87
) and (
4.95
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)-K_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(0)}(x,y;t)\\
&\quad=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}\big((1-\psi_{0}^{L})p_{t^{\prime}}\big)*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x})\Big)(z)\\
&\quad\hphantom{XXXXXX}\times\Big(P(t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y}\Big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz\\
&\quad\quad+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x})\Big)(z)\\
&\quad\hphantom{XXXXXX}\times\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}\big((1-\psi_{0}^{L^{\prime}})p_{t^{\prime}}\big)*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y})\Big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz\\
&\quad=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathcal{S}(t)\big((1-\psi_{0}^{L})p_{t^{\prime}}\big)\Big)(z-x)\\
&\quad\hphantom{XXXXXX}\times\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathcal{S}(t)p_{t^{\prime}}\Big)(z-y)dt^{\prime}dz\\
&\quad\quad+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathcal{S}(t)(\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t}^{\prime})\Big)(z-x)\\
&\quad\hphantom{XXXXXX}\times\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathcal{S}(t)\big((1-\psi_{0}^{L^{\prime}})p_{t^{\prime}}\big)\Big)(z-y)dt^{\prime}dz\\
&\quad=:\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt V}(x,y;t)+\text{V}(x,y;t).\end{split}

(4.96)

We only consider the first term
I V
on the right-hand side of (
4.96
)
since the term
V
can be treated in a similar manner.
Recalling that
\psi_{0}^{L}=1
on
B_{\frac{L}{8}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|e^{-t^{\prime}}(1-\psi_{0}^{L})p_{t^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim e^{-c\frac{L^{2}}{t^{\prime}}-t^{\prime}}\lesssim e^{-cL},

(4.97)

where the second inequality follows from separately estimating
the cases (i)
t^{\prime}\ll L
, (ii)
t^{\prime}\sim L
, and (iii)
t^{\prime}\gg L
.
Moreover, since
\psi_{0}^{L}=1
on
B_{\frac{L}{8}}
,
L\gg R+t
,
and
x,y\in B_{2R}
, we have

\displaystyle|z-y|\geq|z-x|-|x|-|y|\geq\frac{L}{8}-t-4R\geq\frac{L}{16}

in the domain of integration for
I V
in (
4.96
).
Under this condition, we have, as in (
4.84
),

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{\{|z-y|\geq\frac{L}{16}\}}e^{-t^{\prime}}\mathcal{S}(t)p_{t^{\prime}}(z-y)\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\lesssim e^{-cL}.

(4.98)

Then,
by applying Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality
with (
4.97
) and (
4.98
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt V}(x,y;t)|\lesssim e^{-cL}.

(4.99)

A similar computation yields

\displaystyle|\text{V}(x,y;t)|\lesssim e^{-cL^{\prime}}.

(4.100)

Therefore, from (
4.96
), (
4.99
), and (
4.100
),
we conclude that

\displaystyle|K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)-K_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(0)}(x,y;t)|\lesssim e^{-cL}+e^{-cL^{\prime}}.

(4.101)

It remains to estimate
K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)
.
In view of (
4.95
) with (
4.42
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)&=K^{t}(x-y)\\
:\!&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{(\widehat{\mathcal{S}(t)}(\eta))^{2}}{\langle 2\pi\eta\rangle^{2}}(1-e^{-\langle 2\pi\eta\rangle^{2}})e^{2\pi i\eta\cdot(x-y)}d\eta.\end{aligned}

(4.102)

From (
4.42
) with (
4.41
), we have

\displaystyle\big|(\widehat{\mathcal{S}(t)}(\eta))^{2}(1-e^{-\langle 2\pi\eta\rangle^{2}})-e^{-t}\cos^{2}(t[\hskip-1.70717pt[2\pi\eta]\hskip-1.70717pt])\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle\eta\rangle},

(4.103)

uniformly in
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Then, by defining
K^{t}_{1}
by

\displaystyle K^{t}_{1}(z)=e^{-t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\cos^{2}(t[\hskip-1.70717pt[2\pi\eta]\hskip-1.70717pt])}{\langle 2\pi\eta\rangle^{2}}e^{2\pi i\eta\cdot z}d\eta,

(4.104)

it follows from (
4.102
) and (
4.103
) that

\displaystyle|K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)-K_{1}^{t}(x-y)|\lesssim 1,

(4.105)

uniformly in
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

We now estimate
K_{1}^{t}
.
Recall from Appendix B.5 in
[
33
]

that the Fourier transform of a radial function
F(x)=f(|x|)
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}

is given by

\displaystyle\widehat{F}(\eta)=2\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}f(r)J_{0}(2\pi r|\eta|)rdr,

(4.106)

where
J_{0}

is the Bessel function of order zero, satisfying
the following asymptotics as
r\to\infty
:

\displaystyle J_{0}(r)=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi r}}\cos\Big(r-\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)+O(r^{-\frac{3}{2}}).

(4.107)

See Appendix B.8 in
[
33
]

for the asymptotics (
4.107
).
Thus, from (
4.104
) and (
4.106
), we have

\displaystyle K_{1}^{t}(z)=2\pi e^{-t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\cos^{2}(t[\hskip-1.70717pt[2\pi r]\hskip-1.70717pt])}{\langle 2\pi r\rangle^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r|z|)rdr.

(4.108)

Recalling that

J_{0}
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
, it follows from (
4.108
) with (
4.107
)
that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |K_{1}^{t}(z)|&\lesssim e^{-t}\int_{0}^{\max(\frac{1}{|z|},1)}\frac{1}{\langle r\rangle}dr+\int_{\max(\frac{1}{|z|},1)}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|z|^{\frac{1}{2}}}dr\\
&\lesssim 1+(-\log|z|)_{+}.\end{aligned}

(4.109)

Hence, putting together
(
4.86
), (
4.94
),
(
4.101
), (
4.105
), and (
4.109
), we obtain

\displaystyle|K_{L,L^{\prime}}(x,y;t)|+|K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)|\lesssim 1+(-\log|x-y|)_{+}.

(4.110)

Therefore, the claim
(
4.74
) follows
from (
4.86
), (
4.94
), (
4.101
),
and
(
4.110
).
∎

Remark 4.8
.

When
L^{\prime}=\infty
,
we write

\displaystyle K_{L,\infty}(x,y;t)=K_{L,\infty}^{(0)}(x,y;t)+K_{L,\infty}^{(1)}(x,y;t),

(4.111)

where

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K_{L,\infty}^{(0)}(x,y;t)&=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{0}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x})\Big)(z)\\
&\hphantom{XXXXX}\times\big(P(t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y}\big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz\end{aligned}

(4.112)

and

\displaystyle K_{L,\infty}^{(1)}(x,y;t)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}\,e^{-t^{\prime}}(\psi_{j}^{L}p_{t^{\prime}})*(\mathcal{S}(t)(\delta_{x})^{\text{\rm per}}_{L})\Big)(z)

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\big(P(t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y}\big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz.

Proceeding as in (
4.92
), we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{1}_{[-\frac{L}{2},\frac{L}{2})^{2}}(z)\big(P(t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y}\big)(z)\big\|_{H^{-1-\varepsilon}_{z}}\lesssim L^{2}(t^{\prime})^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}.

Then, proceeding as in (
4.93
), we obtain

\displaystyle|K_{L,\infty}^{(1)}(x,y;t)|\lesssim e^{-cL}.

(4.113)

From (
4.95
) and (
4.112
), we have

\displaystyle K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)-K_{L,\infty}^{(0)}(x,y;t)

\displaystyle\quad=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(e^{-t^{\prime}}\big((1-\psi_{0}^{L})p_{t^{\prime}}\big)*(\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{x})\Big)(z)

\displaystyle\quad\hphantom{XXXXXX}\times\Big(P(t^{\prime})\mathcal{S}(t)\delta_{y}\Big)(z)dt^{\prime}dz,

which is precisely

\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt V}(x,y;t)
in (
4.96
).
Hence, from (
4.99
), we have

\displaystyle|K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)-K_{L,\infty}^{(0)}(x,y;t)|\lesssim e^{-cL}.

(4.114)

Therefore, putting (
4.111
),
(
4.113
), and (
4.114
) together,
we obtain

\displaystyle|K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)-K_{L,\infty}(x,y;t)|\lesssim e^{-cL}.

(4.115)

On the other hand, from
(
4.74
) and (
4.115
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|K_{L,L}(x,y;t)|+|K_{L,\infty}(x,y;t)|+|K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)|&\lesssim 1+(-\log|x-y|)_{+},\\
|K_{L,L}(x,y;t)-K_{\infty,\infty}(x,y;t)|&\lesssim e^{-cL}.\end{split}

(4.116)

A computation analogous to
(
4.76
)
with Lemma
2.14
,
(
4.75
) (with
L^{\prime}=\infty
), (
4.115
), and (
4.116
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big[\|:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{L}(1))^{m}\!:-:\!(\mathcal{S}(t)Z_{\infty}(1))^{m}\!:\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p_{1}}(B_{R})}^{r}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim C(R,r)e^{-cL}\longrightarrow 0,

as
L\to\infty
.
This proves (
4.69
).

4.3.
Estimates
on the enhanced data sets

In this subsection,
we establish
the following proposition
on regularities of the enhanced data sets.

Proposition 4.9
.

(i)

Let
R>0
.
Given small
\varepsilon>0
, there exists finite
p=p(\varepsilon)\gg 1
such that
the following holds true.
Suppose that
random
k
-tuple of functions
\Xi_{0},\widetilde{\Xi}_{0}\in\mathbb{W}(R)
are independent of
\Phi_{1}=\Phi_{1}(u_{1},\xi)

defined in (
4.10
),
where
\mathbb{W}(R)
is as in (
4.15
).
Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{u_{1},\xi,\Xi_{0},\widetilde{\Xi}_{0}}\Big[\|\Xi_{1}(u_{1},\xi)\|_{\Xi_{0},\widetilde{\Xi}_{0},R}\Big]\lesssim 1,

where the implicit constant is independent of the distributions
of
\Xi_{0}
and
\widetilde{\Xi}_{0}
.
Here,
\Xi_{1}(u_{1},\xi)
is as in (
4.13
)
and
\|\cdot\|_{\Xi_{0},\widetilde{\Xi}_{0},R}
is as in
Definition
4.1
(iii).

(ii)
Let
\Xi_{0}(\phi)
be as in (
4.12
). Then,
given any
\varepsilon>0
, finite
p\gg 1
, and
\mu>0
,
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{L\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{L}}\Big[\|\Xi_{0}(\phi)\|_{(L^{\infty}_{t}W^{-\varepsilon,p}_{\mu})^{\otimes k}}\Big]<\infty.

(4.117)

We first state and prove an auxiliary lemma.
The proof of Proposition
4.9

is presented at the end of this subsection.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
\Phi_{1}=\Phi_{1}(u_{1},\xi)
be as in (
4.10
),
where
u_{1}
and
\xi

denote the independent spatial and space-time white noises as in (
4.1
).
Define
H(x,y;t)
by

\displaystyle H(x,y;t)=\mathbb{E}\big[\Phi_{1}(x,t)\Phi_{1}(y,t)\big].

(4.118)

Let
R>0
.
Then, given small
\varepsilon>0

and
1\leq q<\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
,
there exists a finite constant
C(R)>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{\varepsilon}\langle\nabla_{y}\rangle^{\varepsilon}H(x,y;t)\|_{L^{q}_{x,y}(B_{R}\times B_{R})}\leq C(R),

(4.119)

uniformly in
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Proof.

With a slight abuse of notation, it follows
from Definition
3.4
and an analogous property
for the space-time white noise
\xi
that

\displaystyle H(x-y;t)

\displaystyle\equiv H(x,y;t)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{D}(t)\delta_{x},\mathcal{D}(t)\delta_{y}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathcal{D}(t-t^{\prime})\delta_{x},\mathcal{D}(t-t^{\prime})\delta_{y}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}dt^{\prime}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{\varepsilon}\langle\nabla_{y}\rangle^{\varepsilon}H(x-y;t)=\langle\nabla_{z}\rangle^{2\varepsilon}H(z;t)|_{z=x-y}.

(4.120)

Define

\widetilde{H}^{t}(z)
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{t}(z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin^{2}(t[\hskip-1.70717pt[2\pi\eta]\hskip-1.70717pt])}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[2\pi\eta]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{2}}e^{2\pi i\eta\cdot z}d\eta.

Then, from (
1.23
), we have

\displaystyle H(z;t)

\displaystyle=e^{-t}\widetilde{H}^{t}(z)+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-t^{\prime}}\widetilde{H}^{t^{\prime}}(z)dt^{\prime}.

(4.121)

In view of (
4.120
) and (
4.121
),
it suffices to study

\langle\nabla\rangle^{2\varepsilon}\widetilde{H}^{t}(z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\langle\eta\rangle^{2\varepsilon}\frac{\sin^{2}(t[\hskip-1.70717pt[2\pi\eta]\hskip-1.70717pt])}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[2\pi\eta]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{2}}e^{2\pi i\eta\cdot z}d\eta.

As the inverse Fourier transform of a radial function,

\langle\nabla\rangle^{2\varepsilon}\widetilde{H}^{t}
is radial.
Hence, from (
4.106
), we have

\displaystyle\langle\nabla\rangle^{2\varepsilon}\widetilde{H}^{t}(z)=2\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}\langle r\rangle^{2\varepsilon}\frac{\sin^{2}(t[\hskip-1.70717pt[2\pi r]\hskip-1.70717pt])}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[2\pi r]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r|z|)rdr,

where
J_{0}

is the Bessel function of order zero.
Using the boundedness of
J_{0}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and (
4.107
),
we then have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\langle\nabla\rangle^{2\varepsilon}\widetilde{H}^{t}(z)|&\lesssim\int_{0}^{\max(\frac{1}{|z|},1)}\frac{1}{\langle r\rangle^{1-2\varepsilon}}dr+\int_{\max(\frac{1}{|z|},1)}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{3}{2}-2\varepsilon}|z|^{\frac{1}{2}}}dr\\
&\lesssim 1+|z|^{-2\varepsilon}.\end{split}

(4.122)

Therefore, from (
4.120
), (
4.121
), and (
4.122
),
we obtain

|\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{\varepsilon}\langle\nabla_{y}\rangle^{\varepsilon}H(x,y;t)|\lesssim 1+|x-y|^{-2\varepsilon},

uniformly in
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Then, by integrating in
x,y\in B_{R}
, we obtain
(
4.119
),
provided that
q<\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
.
∎

We are now ready to present the proof of Proposition
4.9
.

Proof of Proposition
4.9
.

(i)
We only show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\Xi_{1}(u_{1},\xi)\|_{\Xi_{0},R}\big]<\infty,

(4.123)

where
\|\Xi_{1}(u_{1},\xi)\|_{\Xi_{0},R}
is as in Definition
4.1
(ii).
A similar argument yields finiteness of
the expectations
of the constants
K_{2}
and
K_{3}
in (
4.17
) and (
4.18
), respectively.

Let
j=1,\dots,k
.
Given a function
f
on
B_{R}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
let
{\bf f}
an extension of
f
onto
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Given a test function

\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

supported on a ball
B
of radius 1 with
B\subset B_{2R}
, it follows from Lemma
2.13
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\langle{\bf f}:\!\Phi_{1}^{j}(t)\!:\,,\varphi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\int_{(B_{2R})^{2}}{\bf f}(x){\bf f}(y)\varphi(x)\varphi(y)\mathbb{E}\big[:\!\Phi_{1}^{j}(x,t))\!:\,:\!\Phi_{1}^{j}(y,t)\!:\big]dxdy

\displaystyle=j!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}{\bf f}(x){\bf f}(y)\varphi(x)\varphi(y)\big(H(x,y;t)\big)^{j}dxdy,

where
H(x,y;t)
is as in (
4.118
).
Then, given small
\varepsilon>0
,
it follows from the duality, Lemma
2.8
(ii),
the bi-parameter fractional Leibniz rule (Lemma
2.10
),
and Lemma
4.10

that there exist finite
p=p(\varepsilon)\gg 1
and
q=q(\varepsilon)\gg 1

(with
jq<\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\leq\frac{p}{2}
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big[\big|\langle{\bf f}:\!\Phi_{1}^{j}(t)\!:,\varphi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\bf f}\varphi\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,q^{\prime}}(B_{2R})}^{2}\big\|\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{\varepsilon}\langle\nabla_{y}\rangle^{\varepsilon}\big(H(x,y;t)\big)^{j}\big\|_{L^{q}_{x,y}(B_{2R}\times B_{2R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{W^{\varepsilon,q^{\prime}}(B_{2R})}^{2}\|{\bf f}\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p}(B_{2R})}^{2}\|\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{\varepsilon}\langle\nabla_{y}\rangle^{\varepsilon}H(x,y;t)\|^{j}_{L^{jq}_{x,y}(B_{2R}\times B_{2R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{W^{\varepsilon,q^{\prime}}(B_{2R})}^{2}\|{\bf f}\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}.

Hence, by taking an infimum over the extension
{\bf f}

and applying Lemma
2.14
(with
j\leq k
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\,f:\!\Phi^{j}_{1}(t)\!:\|_{W^{-2(k+1)\varepsilon,p}(B_{R})}^{p}\Big]\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p}(B_{R})}^{p},

uniformly in
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Then, by first conditioning on
\Xi_{0}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{\int_{0}^{T}\|:\!\Phi^{j}_{1}(t)\!:\Xi_{0\ell}(t)\|_{W^{-2(k+1)\varepsilon,p}(B_{R})}^{p}dt}{\int_{0}^{T}\|\Xi_{0\ell}(t)\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p}(B_{R})}^{p}dt}\Bigg]\leq A_{j,\ell}<\infty.

Hence, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\Xi_{1}(u_{1},\xi)\|_{\Xi_{0},R}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\max_{0\leq j,\ell\leq k}\frac{\int_{0}^{T}\|:\!\Phi^{j}_{1}(t)\!:\Xi_{0\ell}(t)\|_{W^{-2(k+1)\varepsilon,p}(B_{R})}^{p}dt}{\int_{0}^{T}\|\Xi_{0\ell}(t)\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p}(B_{R})}^{p}dt}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j,\ell=0}^{k}A_{j,\ell}<\infty.

This proves (
4.123
).

(ii)
The bound
(
4.117
)
follows from the proof of Proposition
4.4
.
Namely, in view of
(
4.37
), (
4.39
),
and (
4.60
)
with (
4.40
),
(
4.56
), (
4.57
),
(
4.58
), and (
4.59
),
it follows from
Kolmogorov’s continuity criterion
(
[
5
, Theorem 8.2]
)
together with the exponential decay in time (see
Remark
4.5
)
that

\displaystyle\rho_{L}\Big(\|\Xi_{0}(\phi)\|_{(L^{\infty}_{t}W^{-\varepsilon,p}_{\mu})^{\otimes k}}>K\Big)\lesssim\frac{1}{K^{q}}

for any finite
q\geq 1
,
from which the desired bound (
4.117
) follows.
∎

4.4.
Global well-posedness on the plane

We are now ready to prove global well-posedness
of the hyperbolic
\Phi^{k+1}_{2}
-model (
4.1
)
on the plane.
We do this by constructing a solution
on each cone
\mathbf{C}_{R}
,
R>0
.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
\vec{\rho}_{\infty}
be as in Theorem
1.2
(i)
and

\Law(u_{0},u_{1})=\vec{\rho}_{\infty}
.
Then, given
R>0
,
the hyperbolic
\Phi^{k+1}_{2}
-model (
4.1
)
is well-posed on the cone
\mathbf{C}_{R}
.
More precisely,
there exists
a function
u
of the form (
4.8
)
such that
the following statements holds true

\vec{\rho}_{\infty}
-almost surely
:

•

(u,\partial_{t}u)\in L^{\infty}([0,R];\vec{H}^{-\varepsilon_{k}}(B_{R-t}))
,

•

the remainder term

v=u-\mathcal{S}(t)u_{0}-\mathcal{D}(t)u_{1}-\Psi

satisfies the equation (
4.11
) on
\mathbf{C}_{R}
,
and

(v,\partial_{t}v)\in L^{\infty}([0,R];\vec{H}^{1-\varepsilon_{k}}(B_{R-t}))
,

where
\varepsilon_{k}=2(k+1)\varepsilon
is as in (
4.19
).

For the convergence of the
L
-periodic solution
u_{L}
,
L\in\mathcal{A}
,
to the solution
u
constructed in Proposition
4.11
,
see Remark
4.12
below.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\{L_{j}:j\in\mathbb{N}\}\subset\mathbb{N}

be the index set such that
Theorem
1.2
(i)
and
Proposition
4.6
hold.
Given a spatial white noise
\zeta
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}

and a space-time white noise
\xi
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
let

\zeta_{L}
be the
L
-periodic spatial white noise defined in (
3.53
)
and (
3.54
),
and

\xi_{L}
be the
L
-periodic space-time white noise defined in (
4.4
)
and (
4.5
),
L\geq 1
, respectively.

In Proposition
4.6
,
we proved
that the
L_{j}
-periodic enhanced Gibbs measure

\nu_{L_{j}}=(\Xi_{0})_{\#}\rho_{L_{j}}

on
\mathbb{W}=\big(C(\mathbb{R}_{+};W^{-\varepsilon,p}_{\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))\big)^{\otimes k}

converges weakly to

\nu_{\infty}=(\Xi_{0})_{\#}\rho_{\infty}

as
j\to\infty
.
Then, by
the Skorokhod representation theorem
(Lemma
2.19
),
there exist
a probability space
(\widetilde{\Omega},\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}})
,
and random variables
\Xi_{0}^{j},\Xi_{0}:\widetilde{\Omega}\to\mathbb{W}

such that

\displaystyle\Law(\Xi_{0}^{j})=\nu_{L_{j}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Law(\Xi_{0})=\nu_{\infty},

(4.124)

and
\Xi_{0}^{j}
converges
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely to
\Xi_{0}
in
\mathbb{W}
as
j\to\infty
.
Given
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
we define a transport plan
\mathfrak{p}_{L_{j}}\in\Pi(\nu_{\infty},\nu_{L_{j}})
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{L_{j}}=(\Xi_{0},\Xi_{0}^{j})_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}.

(4.125)

Then, by the bounded convergence theorem, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{W}}d_{\mathbb{W}}(\Theta,\Theta^{\prime})d\mathfrak{p}_{L_{j}}(\Theta,\Theta^{\prime})&=\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}d_{\mathbb{W}}(\Xi_{0}(\widetilde{\omega}),\Xi_{0}^{j}(\widetilde{\omega}))d\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{\omega})\\
&\longrightarrow 0,\end{split}

(4.126)

as
j\to\infty
,
where
the metric
d_{\mathbb{W}}
is as in (
4.14
).
See Remark 6.11 in
[
64
]
.

From (
4.36
) and (
4.61
) with (
4.12
),
we have

\displaystyle\rho_{L_{j}}=(\text{eval}_{t=0}\circ\text{proj}_{1}\big)_{\#}\nu_{L_{j}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\rho_{\infty}=(\text{eval}_{t=0}\circ\text{proj}_{1}\big)_{\#}\nu_{\infty},

(4.127)

where
\text{eval}_{t=0}
denotes the evaluation map at time
t=0

and
\text{proj}_{1}
is the projection onto the first component.
We now define the first components of the initial data
by

Remark 4.12
.

A slight modification of the proof of Proposition
4.11

shows that,
on each cone
\mathbf{C}_{R}
,
R>0
,
the
L_{j}
-periodic solution
\vec{u}_{L_{j}}=(u_{L_{j}},\partial_{t}u_{L_{j}})
to (
4.2
)
converges in probability
to
the solution
\vec{u}=(u,\partial_{t}u)
to (
4.1
) constructed in Proposition
4.11

in the
L^{\infty}([0,R];\vec{H}^{-\varepsilon_{k}}(B_{R-t}))
-topology.
In particular,
there exists a subsequence
\{u_{L_{j_{\ell}}}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}

such that
\vec{u}_{L_{j_{\ell}}}

converges almost surely to

\vec{u}
in

L^{\infty}([0,R];\vec{H}^{-\varepsilon_{k}}(B_{R-t}))

as
\ell\to\infty
.
Since

\vec{u}_{L_{j_{\ell}}}\in C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\vec{H}^{-\varepsilon_{k}}_{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
,
we then deduce that

u\in C(\big[0,\tfrac{R}{2}\big];\vec{H}^{-\varepsilon_{k}}(B_{R/2}))

almost surely.
Since the choice of
R>0
was arbitrary,
we conclude that

(u,\partial_{t}u)\in C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\vec{H}^{-\varepsilon_{k}}_{\textup{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))

almost surely.

Let
u_{0}
and
u_{0,L_{j}}
be as in (
4.128
).
Then,
define a set

by

m\geq\delta^{*}(\mu,R,M_{0},M_{1},M_{2})^{-1}\end{subarray}}\bigg(\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\bigcup_{j=\ell}^{\infty}\big(D_{R,M_{0},M_{1},M_{2}}^{L_{j}}\cap C_{R,\frac{1}{m}}^{L_{j}}\big)\Big\}\bigg),\end{split}

(4.147)

where

(see also (

)).

) and (

),

\kappa>0

was arbitrary,
we conclude that

With a slight abuse of notation, let us denote by
\omega^{\prime}

(4.149)

,

(4.150)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\otimes\mathbb{P}_{u_{1},\xi}\Big(\big\|\vec{u}_{L_{j_{1}}}(u_{0,L_{j_{1}}}(\widetilde{\omega}),u_{1},\xi)-\vec{u}_{L_{j_{2}}}(u_{0,L_{j_{2}}}(\widetilde{\omega}),u_{1},\xi)\big\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R];\vec{H}^{-\varepsilon_{k}}(B_{R-t}))}>\delta\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\Big(d_{\mathbb{W}}\big(\Xi_{0}(u_{0,L_{j_{1}}}),\Xi_{0}(u_{0,L_{j_{2}}})\big)>\delta_{0}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\otimes\mathbb{P}_{u_{1},\xi}\Big((D_{R,M_{0},M_{1},M_{2}}^{L_{j_{1}}})^{c}\cup(D_{R,M_{0},M_{1},M_{2}}^{L_{j_{2}}})^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad<\kappa

for any
j_{2}\geq j_{1}\gg 1
.
Here,

\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\delta)>0
is a small number
such that

d_{\mathbb{W}}(f,g)\leq\delta_{0}

implies
\|f-g\|_{\mathbb{W}(R)}\leq\frac{1}{2}C_{0}^{-1}\delta
,
where

C_{0}=C_{0}(R,M_{0},M_{1},M_{2})>0

are as in Proposition
4.3
.
This shows that

\{\vec{u}_{L_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}

is Cauchy in probability
in
L^{\infty}([0,R];\vec{H}^{-\varepsilon_{k}}(B_{R-t}))
.

4.5.
Invariance of the Gibbs measure
\vec{\rho}_{\infty}

We conclude this section by briefly discussing invariance of the Gibbs measure
\vec{\rho}_{\infty}

under the dynamics of the hyperbolic
\Phi^{k+1}_{2}
-model (
4.1
) on the plane.
We only consider the values of
L
belonging to
\mathcal{A}=\{L_{j}:j\in\mathbb{N}\}\subset\mathbb{N}
,
along which we proved Theorem
1.2
(i).

Let
u
be the solution to
the hyperbolic
\Phi^{k+1}_{2}
-model (
4.1
)
with

\Law(u_{0},u_{1})=\vec{\rho}_{\infty}
.
Our goal is to show

\displaystyle\Law(\vec{u}(t))=\vec{\rho}_{\infty}

(4.151)

for each
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Fix
t>0
.
Given
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
u_{L_{j}}
be the solution to
the
L_{j}
-periodic hyperbolic
\Phi^{k+1}_{2}
-model (
4.2
)
with

\Law(u_{0,L_{j}},u_{1,L_{j}})=\vec{\rho}_{L_{j}}

such that

(u_{0,L_{j}},u_{1,L_{j}})
converges almost surely to

(u_{0},u_{1})

in
\vec{H}_{\textup{loc}}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, as
j\to\infty
.
By the invariance of the Gibbs measure

\vec{\rho}_{L_{j}}
under the
L_{j}
-periodic dynamics,
we have

\Law(\vec{u}_{L_{j}}(t))=\vec{\rho}_{L_{j}}

for any
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Then, it follows from the weak convergence
of
\{\vec{\rho}_{L_{j}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
to
\vec{\rho}_{\infty}

that

\displaystyle\vec{\rho}_{\infty}=\wlim_{j\to\infty}\Law(\vec{u}_{L_{j}}(t)),

(4.152)

where
\wlim
denotes
a weak limit of probability measures.

On the other hand,
given
R>t
, let
\vec{\varphi}=(\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1})\in(\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))^{\otimes 2}=(C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))^{\otimes 2}

be a pair of test functions with
\supp\varphi_{\ell}\subset B_{R}
,
\ell=1,2
.
Then, it follows from the proof of Proposition
4.11

(see Remark
4.12
)
that the solution
\vec{u}_{L_{j}}
to (
4.2
)
converges in probability to the solution
\vec{u}
to (
4.1
)
on

\mathbf{C}_{R+t}
as
j\to\infty
.
This in particular implies

\langle\vec{u}_{L_{j}}(t),\vec{\varphi}\rangle

converges in probability to

\langle\vec{u}(t),\vec{\varphi}\rangle

as
j\to\infty
,
where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes

(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))^{\otimes 2}
-
(\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))^{\otimes 2}

duality pairing.
In particular,
as a
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))^{\otimes 2}
-valued random variable

\vec{u}_{L_{j}}(t)
converges in law to
\vec{u}(t)
and,
therefore, together with (
4.152
)
and the uniqueness of a limit,
we obtain (
4.151
).
This concludes the proof of Theorem
1.2
(ii).

Appendix A
Embeddings between weighted Sobolev and Besov spaces

In this appendix, we present the proof of Lemma
2.3
.
Fix
1\leq p<\infty
.
We first prove

\displaystyle\|f\|_{W^{s,p}_{\mu}}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{s^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}}_{p,1}}.

(A.1)

for
s<s^{\prime}
and
\mu\geq c(p)\mu^{\prime}>0
.
Let us state an auxiliary lemma.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
, finite
p\geq 1
, and
\mu>0
.
Then, given any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
c_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\phi_{j}\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{W^{s,p}}\lesssim e^{c_{0}\frac{\mu}{p}2^{j\delta}}2^{(s+\varepsilon)k}\|\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{L^{p}_{\mu}}

(A.2)

for any
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
where
\mathbf{Q}_{k}
is as in (
2.8
).

We first prove (
A.1
) by assuming Lemma
A.1
.
We present the proof of Lemma
A.1

at the end of this section.
From
(
2.23
) and Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{W^{s,p}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{W^{s,p}_{\mu}}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\mu}{p}2^{j\delta}}\|\phi_{j}\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{W^{s,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\mu}{p}2^{j\delta}}e^{c_{0}\frac{\mu^{\prime}}{p}2^{j\delta}}2^{(s+\varepsilon)k}\|\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{L^{p}_{\mu^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{s^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}}_{p,1}},

provided that
\mu>c_{0}\mu^{\prime}

and
s<s^{\prime}
.
This proves (
A.1
).

Next, we prove

\displaystyle\|f\|_{B^{s,\mu}_{p,\infty}}\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{s,p}_{\mu^{\prime}}}

(A.3)

for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
and

0<\mu^{\prime}<c_{1}\mu

for some small
c_{1}>0
.

From (
2.17
) and (
2.18
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}2^{sk}\|\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{L^{p}_{\mu}}&\leq\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}2^{sk}\|\phi_{j^{\prime}}\mathbf{Q}_{k}(\phi_{j}f)\|_{L^{p}_{\mu}}\\
&\leq\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}e^{-c\frac{\mu}{p}2^{j^{\prime}\delta}}2^{sk}\|\phi_{j^{\prime}}\mathbf{Q}_{k}(\phi_{j}f)\|_{L^{p}}.\end{split}

(A.4)

Note that
we have

\displaystyle\big(\phi_{j^{\prime}}\mathbf{Q}_{k}(\phi_{j}f)\big)(x)=\phi_{j^{\prime}}(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\eta_{k}(x-y)(\phi_{j}f)(y)dy,

(A.5)

where
\eta_{k}
is as in (
2.9
).
In view of (
2.16
),
we have
|x|\sim 2^{j^{\prime}}
and
|y|\sim 2^{j}

in the integration above.

We first estimate the contribution to (
A.4
) from the case
j^{\prime}\geq j-2
.
By first summing over
j^{\prime}

and applying Bernstein’s inequality and (
2.23
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j^{\prime}=j-2}^{\infty}e^{-c\frac{\mu}{p}2^{j^{\prime}\delta}}2^{sk}\|\phi_{j^{\prime}}\mathbf{Q}_{k}(\phi_{j}f)\|_{L^{p}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\mu}{p}2^{j\delta}}2^{sk}\|\mathbf{Q}_{k}(\phi_{j}f)\|_{L^{p}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\mu}{p}2^{j\delta}}e^{\frac{\mu^{\prime}}{p}2^{j\delta}}\|f\|_{W^{s,p}_{\mu^{\prime}}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{s,p}_{\mu^{\prime}}},\end{split}

(A.6)

uniformly in
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
provided that
0<\mu^{\prime}<c^{\prime}\mu
.

Next, we consider the case
j^{\prime}\leq j-3
.
In this case, we have
|x-y|\sim|y|\sim 2^{j}
in (
A.5
).
Then, it follows from (
2.10
) that

\displaystyle|\eta_{k}(x-y)|\lesssim 2^{dk}e^{-c|2^{k}(x-y)|^{\frac{1}{\theta_{0}}}}\lesssim 2^{dk}e^{-c^{\prime}|2^{k}\cdot 2^{j}|^{\frac{1}{\theta_{0}}}}e^{-c^{\prime}|2^{k}(x-y)|^{\frac{1}{\theta_{0}}}}.

(A.7)

In the following,
it is understood that, given
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
we have
|x-y|\sim|y|\sim 2^{j}
in (
A.5
).
Hence,
by first summing over
j^{\prime}

and applying Bernstein’s inequality followed by Young’s inequality with (
A.7
)
and (
2.23
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{j-3}e^{-c\frac{\mu}{p}2^{j^{\prime}\delta}}2^{sk}\|\phi_{j^{\prime}}(\eta_{k}*(\phi_{j}f))\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\|\eta_{k}*\langle\nabla\rangle^{s}(\phi_{j}f)\|_{L^{p}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-c^{\prime\prime}|2^{k}\cdot 2^{j}|^{\frac{1}{\theta_{0}}}}\|\phi_{j}f\|_{W^{s,p}}\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-c^{\prime\prime}|2^{k}\cdot 2^{j}|^{\frac{1}{\theta_{0}}}}e^{\frac{\mu^{\prime}}{p}2^{j\delta}}\|f\|_{W^{s,p}_{\mu^{\prime}}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{s,p}_{\mu^{\prime}}}\end{split}

(A.8)

for any
\mu^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
since we have
0<\delta<\frac{1}{\theta_{0}}
;
see
the definition (
2.11
)
of the weight
w_{\mu}
.

Putting (
A.4
), (
A.6
), and (
A.8
)
together, we obtain (
A.3
).

We conclude this paper by presenting the proof of Lemma
A.1
.

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

We first consider the case
s\leq 0
.
From (
2.23
), we have

\displaystyle\|\phi_{j}\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{W^{s,p}}\leq\|\phi_{j}\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{L^{p}}\leq e^{\frac{\mu}{p}a_{1}2^{j\delta}}\|\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{L^{p}_{\mu}}

(A.9)

for any
\mu>0
,
uniformly in
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
By (
2.27
) and Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\phi_{j}\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{W^{s,p}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{W^{s,p}}\lesssim 2^{sk}\|\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{L^{p}},

(A.10)

uniformly in
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
Then, (
A.2
) follows from interpolating (
A.9
) and (
A.10
);
see
[
7
, Theorem 5.4.1]

for an interpolation of weighted Lebesgue spaces.

Next, we consider the case
s>0
.
In this case, (
A.2
) follows from interpolating

\displaystyle\|\phi_{j}\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{L^{p}}\leq e^{\frac{\mu}{p}a_{1}2^{j\delta}}\|\mathbf{Q}_{k}f\|_{L^{p}_{\mu}}

and (
A.10
) (with
s
replaced by
s+\varepsilon
).
∎
